Table 4
Q&A General Knowledge
1. Q: How would you design a financial workflow that creates a single source of truth across programs and finance systems?
A: Show answer
I would design the workflow around one controlled financial data model rather than around one spreadsheet owned by one person. Each program and finance system should continue to capture information for its own operational purpose, but the reporting layer should standardize those inputs into common fields such as transaction ID, date, project code, donor code, account code, funding source, department, amount, currency, vendor, approval status, and supporting-document status. Power Query can connect to the different source files or systems, standardize column names and data types, clean duplicates, and load the results into a controlled reporting model. The important design principle is that the reporting model becomes the agreed analytical source, while original systems remain the source records for their transactions. Excel tables can provide stable structured data areas, and VBA can coordinate refreshes, validations, exception reports, reconciliations, and controlled outputs. Microsoft notes that Power Query performs data acquisition and transformation but does not itself define a single storage destination; the host application determines where transformed data is loaded. That means the workflow should explicitly define the authoritative destination and governance around it rather than assuming that Power Query alone creates a single source of truth.

Study instruction:
- "Understand the difference between source systems, the controlled reporting model, and the final management reports."
Key point:
- A single source of truth is a governed data model, not simply one file.
- Source records, transformation rules, controls, and ownership must be clearly defined.
Official links:
- What Is Power Query? — Microsoft Learn
- Excel VBA reference — Microsoft Learn
2. Q: What does "single source of truth" actually mean in an NGO finance environment?
A: Show answer
In an NGO, a single source of truth means that management, programs, and Finance use the same approved definitions, transaction population, coding structure, and reporting logic when discussing financial performance. It does not necessarily mean that every transaction must be entered into one spreadsheet. A bank system, accounting system, procurement register, payroll system, and program tracker may remain separate operational sources. The single source of truth is created when those sources are integrated into a controlled model with documented rules for which source is authoritative for each field. For example, the accounting system may be authoritative for posted expense, procurement may be authoritative for purchase-order status, and a grant register may be authoritative for donor restrictions. The reporting model then brings those facts together without silently replacing the original records. This distinction prevents a common mistake: treating a manually maintained dashboard as if it were the underlying accounting system.

Study instruction:
- "Learn to define the authoritative source for every important financial field before building reports."
Key point:
- One source of truth means one governed interpretation of the data.
- Different systems can remain operationally separate while feeding one controlled reporting model.
Official links:
- Power Query documentation — Microsoft Learn
- Overview of Excel tables — Microsoft Support
3. Q: Why is a central data model better than asking every program to maintain its own reporting spreadsheet?
A: Show answer
Separate program spreadsheets often develop different account labels, project-code formats, date conventions, formulas, and interpretations of commitments or actual expenditure. Finance then spends time consolidating and correcting the differences rather than analyzing the underlying performance. A central data model establishes common fields and transformation rules so that program information can be combined consistently. Programs can still retain local working files for operational management, but those files should feed controlled tables or system extracts rather than become independent reporting versions. This approach also makes it easier to identify when two programs are reporting the same transaction differently. Power Query is designed for importing and reshaping data from multiple sources, while Excel tables provide structured ranges that expand as data changes. The result is a repeatable consolidation process rather than a monthly exercise in copying and pasting.

Study instruction:
- "Review where multiple program spreadsheets are duplicating the same financial information and decide which data should be centralized."
Key point:
- Centralized modelling reduces conflicting spreadsheets.
- Local program tools can remain, but their outputs should feed controlled reporting structures.
Official links:
- Power Query documentation — Microsoft Learn
- Overview of Excel tables — Microsoft Support
4. Q: How would you identify the systems that should feed the financial single source of truth?
A: Show answer
Start by mapping the entire financial information flow rather than beginning with the spreadsheet. Identify where transactions originate, where they are approved, where they are recorded, where supporting documents are stored, and where management reports are produced. In a typical NGO this may include the general ledger, bank records, payroll, procurement, grants, budgets, partner advances, staff advances, inventory, assets, and program activity records. For each source, document what data it owns, how often it changes, who is responsible for it, and how it can be extracted. Then determine whether the source is transactional, reference data, planning data, or supporting evidence. Power Query can connect to multiple data sources and apply consistent transformations. The important control is not simply connectivity; it is documenting which system is authoritative for each field and which transformation is allowed before the information reaches the consolidated model.

Study instruction:
- "Create a source-system map showing data owner, purpose, update frequency, and authoritative fields for each system."
Key point:
- Source mapping should precede automation.
- Every important field needs a clearly identified owner and authoritative source.
Official links:
- Power Query Get Data overview — Microsoft Learn
- What Is Power Query? — Microsoft Learn
5. Q: Which data fields should be standardized across programs and Finance?
A: Show answer
The minimum common structure should normally include a unique transaction or document identifier, transaction date, accounting period, organization or office, program or project code, donor or funding source, account code, cost category, currency, amount, vendor or payee, description, approval status, document status, and source-system identifier. Additional fields may be required for restricted grants, budget lines, procurement references, partner transactions, or intercompany activity. The goal is not to create a giant table containing every possible field. It is to define a practical common core and then add specialized fields where the business process requires them. Validation lists should be used for controlled codes such as project, donor, account, department, and status. Excel's data validation functions can constrain entries, while structured tables help preserve consistent columns as records grow.

Study instruction:
- "Draft a common financial data dictionary and distinguish mandatory fields from process-specific fields."
Key point:
- Standardization should focus on shared fields with clear business meaning.
- Controlled codes are more reliable than unrestricted free text.
Official links:
- Apply data validation — Microsoft Support
- Using structured references with Excel tables — Microsoft Support
6. Q: Why are master data lists essential to a single source of truth?
A: Show answer
A transaction model is only as consistent as the reference data used to classify it. If one program uses "EDU-01," another uses "Education 1," and Finance uses an internal numeric code, reports will require manual interpretation before they can be combined. Master lists for projects, donors, accounts, vendors, departments, currencies, funding restrictions, and status codes provide the common vocabulary. The reporting workflow should validate incoming data against these lists and flag unknown values rather than silently creating new categories. Power Query can merge transaction data with controlled reference tables, while Excel data validation can reduce invalid entries at the input stage. The master data itself should have an owner, effective dates where relevant, and a documented change process so that historical transactions are not unintentionally reclassified when a new project or donor is added.

Study instruction:
- "Build master lists before attempting to automate consolidation across programs."
Key point:
- Master data provides the common language of the financial model.
- Changes to reference data need ownership and historical-control rules.
Official links:
- Apply data validation — Microsoft Support
- Power Query documentation — Microsoft Learn
7. Q: How would you prevent different programs from using different definitions for the same financial concept?
A: Show answer
Establish a finance data dictionary that defines terms such as actual expenditure, commitment, advance, accrual, prepayment, restricted funding, unrestricted funding, budget, forecast, and available balance. Each definition should explain what is included, which source system provides the amount, and how the figure is calculated. For example, "actual expenditure" should normally refer to the organization's agreed accounting basis, while "commitment" may refer to approved but not yet posted obligations. A program dashboard should not invent its own version of these measures. The central reporting model should contain the agreed calculation logic and expose it consistently to every program. This is a governance issue as much as a technical issue because users can have perfectly functioning spreadsheets that produce conflicting numbers if the business definitions are different.

Study instruction:
- "Create written definitions for every management metric used in the consolidated financial reporting pack."
Key point:
- Consistent definitions matter as much as consistent formulas.
- A common data dictionary prevents different teams from using the same term to mean different things.
Official links:
- What Is Power Query? — Microsoft Learn
- Excel object model overview — Microsoft Learn
8. Q: What role should Power Query play in the financial workflow?
A: Show answer
Power Query should normally sit in the ingestion and transformation layer. It can connect to source files or systems, import the required fields, standardize column names, convert data types, remove unwanted records, normalize dates and codes, combine files, merge reference tables, and create a repeatable dataset for reporting. This reduces the need for users to copy and paste raw transactions into a master workbook every month. Power Query is particularly useful when several programs use files with consistent structure but different contents. The queries can define the transformation logic once and repeat it during refresh. The workflow should still retain controls around source approval, refresh timing, exceptions, and reconciliation. Power Query is a transformation engine; Microsoft explicitly notes that its results are loaded into a destination determined by the host product rather than Power Query itself acting as a universal database.

Study instruction:
- "Separate data ingestion and transformation tasks from accounting judgments and final management reporting."
Key point:
- Power Query is well suited to repeatable data preparation and consolidation.
- The organization still needs a governed destination and control framework around the transformed data.
Official links:
- What Is Power Query? — Microsoft Learn
- Power Query Get Data overview — Microsoft Learn
9. Q: What role should VBA play in a multi-program financial workflow?
A: Show answer
VBA should be used for controlled workflow actions that benefit from automation, rather than as a substitute for sound data architecture. Appropriate uses include launching a refresh sequence, checking whether required inputs exist, validating key fields, creating exception lists, generating journal templates, preparing reconciliation summaries, exporting approved reports, writing audit-log entries, and archiving outputs. VBA can also guide users through a defined process so that critical steps are performed in the correct order. It should not be responsible for silently changing accounting classifications or overriding exceptions without review. Microsoft's Excel VBA documentation describes an object model through which code can manipulate workbooks, worksheets, ranges, tables, and other Excel objects. That makes VBA useful as an orchestration layer around an established model, provided the code is documented, tested, and subject to access and security controls.

Study instruction:
- "Use VBA for controlled workflow automation while keeping core accounting definitions visible and reviewable."
Key point:
- VBA should automate repeatable actions, not replace financial judgment.
- Macro logic needs documentation, testing, and controlled distribution.
Official links:
- Excel VBA reference — Microsoft Learn
- Excel object model overview — Microsoft Learn
10. Q: Should the single source of truth be one Excel workbook?
A: Show answer
Not necessarily. For a smaller NGO, a well-designed Excel solution may be sufficient, but the concept should be separated from the tool. A workbook can consolidate approved data, calculations, controls, and reporting, but it becomes fragile if it is simultaneously used as the transaction system, document store, master-data database, reporting engine, and archive. A better design defines the authoritative layers first and then chooses the appropriate technology for each layer. Excel can serve as a controlled analytical and reporting environment; Power Query can prepare data; source systems can remain authoritative for transactions; and a more formal database or platform can become appropriate as volume, users, security requirements, and integration complexity increase. The objective is consistency and traceability, not forcing every business process into one file.

Study instruction:
- "Design the information architecture first and choose the Excel, database, or system component that fits each layer."
Key point:
- A single source of truth is an architecture principle, not an Excel requirement.
- Avoid turning one workbook into an uncontrolled replacement for every source system.
Official links:
- Power Query documentation — Microsoft Learn
- Overview of Excel tables — Microsoft Support
11. Q: How should the workflow distinguish source data from transformed data?
A: Show answer
Keep the raw source layer separate from the transformed and reporting layers. Raw source data should preserve what was received from the originating system, including source identifiers and extraction dates where useful. Transformation logic should then standardize the data without destroying the ability to trace a result back to the original record. The reporting layer should contain the standardized fields and calculated measures used by management. This separation makes troubleshooting easier because Finance can determine whether an error originated in the source file, the transformation rule, the master-data mapping, or the report calculation. It also reduces the temptation to correct a source-data problem by manually editing the consolidated output. A strong workflow therefore moves information forward through controlled stages instead of allowing users to repair the final report by overwriting numbers.

Study instruction:
- "Map the path from raw source record to final report and retain enough identifiers to trace each stage."
Key point:
- Do not overwrite source data to fix reporting problems.
- Data lineage makes errors easier to investigate and correct.
Official links:
- What Is Power Query? — Microsoft Learn
- Power Query Get Data overview — Microsoft Learn
12. Q: Why is a unique transaction identifier important when consolidating program and Finance data?
A: Show answer
A unique identifier provides the anchor for matching information across systems. The same transaction may appear in a bank statement, general ledger, procurement record, payment voucher, donor report, and program tracker. If each system uses an unrelated reference, matching becomes dependent on amount, date, description, or human memory. Those fields can change or contain duplicates. A controlled transaction ID or a reliable composite key makes it easier to identify duplicates, link supporting records, and trace corrections. Where a source system already has a reliable unique identifier, the reporting model should preserve it rather than inventing a second identifier without purpose. When a composite key is required, the logic should be documented and tested against repeated invoices, split payments, partial settlements, and reversals.

Study instruction:
- "Test transaction keys against duplicates, split transactions, reversals, and repeated supplier references."
Key point:
- Traceability depends on stable identifiers.
- Matching should not rely only on amount and date because those values can legitimately repeat.
Official links:
- What Is Power Query? — Microsoft Learn
- Using structured references with Excel tables — Microsoft Support
13. Q: How would you reconcile program records to the finance system?
A: Show answer
Establish a recurring reconciliation between the operational transaction population and the accounting population. First confirm that both datasets cover the same period and scope. Then normalize references, dates, currencies, project codes, and transaction types. Match records using a controlled hierarchy: exact transaction ID where available, then approved composite keys or other documented matching logic. Produce three categories: matched items, finance-only items, and program-only items. Investigate differences rather than forcing them to match. Program-only items may represent transactions that have occurred operationally but are not yet posted; finance-only items may include accounting adjustments, accruals, allocations, or transactions entered without corresponding program detail. The reconciliation should be signed off by responsible staff and exceptions tracked to resolution rather than simply removed from the report.

Study instruction:
- "Build a reconciliation schedule that clearly separates matched records from unresolved differences."
Key point:
- Reconciliation validates completeness and classification across systems.
- Unmatched items require explanation, evidence, and ownership.
Official links:
- Power Query documentation — Microsoft Learn
- Excel VBA reference — Microsoft Learn
14. Q: How should budget information be integrated into the single source of truth?
A: Show answer
Budget should be held as a controlled planning dataset rather than embedded separately inside every report. Each budget line should have a defined period, project, donor or funding source, account or cost category, and approved amount. If budgets are revised, the workflow should preserve the original approved budget and record subsequent revisions with approval dates and reasons. Actual expenditure should then be linked to the same coding structure so that budget-versus-actual analysis uses common dimensions. Commitments and forecasts can be added as separate measures rather than being mixed into actual expenditure. This allows management to distinguish historical spending from expected future use of funds. Power Query can bring approved budget files into the common model, while structured Excel tables can support stable calculations and reporting as new periods or revisions are added.

Study instruction:
- "Keep original budget, approved revisions, actual expenditure, commitments, and forecast as distinct but connected datasets."
Key point:
- Budget and actuals should share common coding dimensions.
- Approved budget changes must remain traceable rather than overwriting the original baseline.
Official links:
- Power Query documentation — Microsoft Learn
- Using structured references with Excel tables — Microsoft Support
15. Q: Why should commitments be included even though they are not yet actual expenditure?
A: Show answer
A financial workflow that reports only posted expenses can give programs a misleading view of available resources. A program may show 60 percent of its budget spent while another 25 percent is already committed through approved purchase orders, contracts, or other obligations. The management decision is different once those commitments are considered. The central model should therefore distinguish actuals, commitments, and remaining budget rather than adding them together as though they were the same accounting measure. The commitment definition must be agreed because procurement requests, approved purchase orders, signed contracts, and informal intentions are not necessarily equivalent. Programs and Finance should be clear about which stages count as commitments for management reporting. This improves forecasting and reduces the risk of approving new expenditure based on an inflated view of available funds.

Study instruction:
- "Define precisely which procurement or contract stages are included in the management commitment figure."
Key point:
- Actual expenditure and commitments answer different management questions.
- Available budget should be assessed using an agreed commitment definition.
Official links:
- What Is Power Query? — Microsoft Learn
- Power Query Get Data overview — Microsoft Learn
16. Q: How would you integrate procurement data with accounting data without double-counting expenditure?
A: Show answer
Procurement and accounting describe different stages of the same economic activity, so the model must keep them distinct. A purchase request is not the same as a purchase order, a purchase order is not the same as an invoice, and an invoice is not necessarily the same as posted expense. Link the records through purchase-order number, invoice number, vendor, project, and other identifiers, but do not add every stage together as if each were a separate cost. The central workflow should show procurement status alongside accounting status. This enables management to see commitments and pipeline while maintaining the accounting system as the authoritative source for posted actuals. Exception reporting can identify purchase orders that have no invoice, invoices that have no purchase order where one is expected, and payments that cannot be linked to supporting procurement records.

Study instruction:
- "Map the procurement-to-payment lifecycle and identify which stage contributes to actual expenditure reporting."
Key point:
- Procurement stages must not be confused with accounting transactions.
- Linking records is different from adding their values together.
Official links:
- Power Query documentation — Microsoft Learn
- Excel VBA reference — Microsoft Learn
17. Q: How should payroll data be integrated into the common financial model?
A: Show answer
Payroll should be imported at an appropriate level of detail while respecting confidentiality and the organization's access controls. The financial model normally needs salary expense, benefits, payroll taxes or statutory obligations where applicable, project or department allocation, accounting period, and payment or accrual status. Program allocations should be based on an approved allocation method rather than informal manual adjustments in the reporting dashboard. Payroll totals should reconcile to the accounting entries, and payroll-related accruals should be distinguished from actual cash payments. Where employees work across programs, the allocation rule should be documented and consistently applied. The model should expose enough information for budget and performance analysis without unnecessarily distributing sensitive individual salary information to users who do not need it.

Study instruction:
- "Separate payroll confidentiality from financial accountability and document how salary costs are allocated to programs."
Key point:
- Payroll integration requires both financial reconciliation and appropriate access controls.
- Allocation rules should be documented and consistently applied.
Official links:
- What Is Power Query? — Microsoft Learn
- Excel object model overview — Microsoft Learn
18. Q: How should grants and donor restrictions be represented in the consolidated model?
A: Show answer
Grant information should be connected to the common transaction model through donor, award, project, budget, funding restriction, period, and reporting requirements. A grant register should distinguish approved funding, receipts, recognized income where applicable, expenditure, commitments, advances, and remaining balances. Restricted funding should not be treated as interchangeable with unrestricted resources merely because the cash sits in the same bank account. The reporting model should therefore retain the funding attributes required to determine whether expenditure is eligible and whether funding remains available. Each grant should have a documented coding structure so that Finance can identify transactions attributed to the award and program managers can see the same figures. The single source of truth then becomes the common basis for donor reports, management reports, and internal budget monitoring without creating separate competing versions.

Study instruction:
- "Link grant metadata to transactions and preserve donor restrictions as controlled attributes rather than free-text notes."
Key point:
- Funding restrictions belong in the data model, not only in narrative documents.
- Grant reporting should reconcile to the same underlying transactions used for management reporting.
Official links:
- Power Query documentation — Microsoft Learn
- Overview of Excel tables — Microsoft Support
19. Q: How would you handle multiple currencies in a single source of truth?
A: Show answer
Preserve the original transaction currency and amount, then calculate reporting-currency values using a documented exchange-rate policy. Do not overwrite the original amount merely because management reports are presented in one base currency. The model should identify the transaction date, rate type, exchange rate used, and reporting conversion where those details are required. Month-end retranslation should be separated from transaction-time conversion so exchange differences are not hidden inside ordinary expenditure. The workflow should also distinguish source-system amounts from management reporting conversions. This allows Finance to reconcile the local-currency accounting records while management can review consolidated figures. When several programs use different currencies, common currency rules become a fundamental part of the single source of truth because otherwise a program total can change simply because users applied different exchange rates.

Study instruction:
- "Document the exchange-rate source, date, and calculation rule used for every reporting currency conversion."
Key point:
- Preserve original currency values and report conversions separately.
- Consistent FX rules are necessary for comparable program reporting.
Official links:
- What Is Power Query? — Microsoft Learn
- Excel VBA reference — Microsoft Learn
20. Q: How should intercompany and interoffice transactions be handled in the common model?
A: Show answer
Internal transfers, cross-office payments, shared services, and other intercompany activity should have dedicated transaction types and counterparty fields. The model should make it possible to identify the sending office, receiving office, original transaction, internal balance, and settlement status. An internal transfer should not automatically become external income or expense simply because it appears in a bank statement. Likewise, when one office pays a cost on behalf of another, the receiving or responsible office should be identifiable so the organization can eliminate or reclassify the internal movement appropriately during consolidation. The single source of truth should therefore contain both local accounting treatment and the internal relationship between offices. Exception reports can highlight balances that have remained unmatched or unsettled beyond the expected timeframe.

Study instruction:
- "Create an internal-transaction code structure and reconcile reciprocal interoffice balances regularly."
Key point:
- Internal cash movement and external financial performance are different concepts.
- Counterparty information is necessary for reliable interoffice reconciliation.
Official links:
- What Is Power Query? — Microsoft Learn
- Excel VBA reference — Microsoft Learn
21. Q: What controls should exist before data enters the consolidated financial model?
A: Show answer
Input controls should include approved templates, required fields, valid project and account codes, date checks, numeric checks, duplicate detection, and clear ownership of source data. Where users enter information manually, validation lists can prevent unsupported codes and inconsistent status values. Files should also have agreed naming and storage rules so the automation knows which files are current and which are historical. A source should be considered ready only when required approvals or reviews have been completed. Power Query can then perform additional structural checks during ingestion, such as identifying missing columns or invalid data types. The central principle is to prevent low-quality input from being disguised as authoritative output. Automated controls should flag problems for review rather than silently replacing questionable values with assumptions.

Study instruction:
- "Design controls at the point of entry so errors are detected before the data reaches management reporting."
Key point:
- Prevention at input stage is stronger than correcting errors after consolidation.
- Validation and source-approval controls should operate before reporting calculations begin.
Official links:
- Apply data validation — Microsoft Support
- Power Query documentation — Microsoft Learn
22. Q: How would you detect duplicate transactions before they distort consolidated reporting?
A: Show answer
Define a duplicate key appropriate to the transaction type and test for repeated records before the data reaches management totals. The key might include source system, transaction ID, invoice number, vendor, date, amount, and project, but it should be tailored to the business process. Some repeated values are legitimate, such as recurring payroll or installment payments, so a duplicate test should flag suspicious repetition rather than automatically delete every matching row. Power Query can identify duplicates during transformation and prepare an exception dataset for review. VBA can present those exceptions in a controlled review sheet or block report generation until required checks are completed. The organization should retain the original rows and document the reason for clearing a false positive so that the control itself is auditable.

Study instruction:
- "Test duplicate logic using both genuine duplicates and legitimate repeated transactions before deployment."
Key point:
- Duplicate detection should flag risk, not blindly delete records.
- The duplicate rule must reflect the structure of the underlying transaction process.
Official links:
- What Is Power Query? — Microsoft Learn
- Excel VBA reference — Microsoft Learn
23. Q: How should exceptions be managed in a single-source-of-truth workflow?
A: Show answer
Exceptions should be treated as a formal workflow rather than as notes hidden in emails. The consolidated model should identify missing codes, unmatched transactions, duplicate candidates, unsupported dates, invalid project assignments, missing documents, unusual amounts, and stale balances. Each exception should have a status, owner, date raised, required action, and resolution date where appropriate. Program teams should be responsible for operational explanations they own, while Finance should remain responsible for accounting treatment and reconciliation. VBA can generate or update an exception register, while Power Query can refresh the underlying records. The workflow should distinguish warnings that require review from errors that prevent a report from being finalized. This keeps automation from becoming an uncontrolled process that forces questionable transactions into the final numbers just to complete the refresh.

Study instruction:
- "Create an exception register with ownership and resolution status instead of correcting unusual records directly in the output report."
Key point:
- Exceptions should be visible, assigned, and resolved through controlled review.
- Automation should surface uncertainty rather than hide it.
Official links:
- What Is Power Query? — Microsoft Learn
- Getting started with VBA in Office — Microsoft Learn
24. Q: How would you reconcile the consolidated model to the general ledger?
A: Show answer
The general ledger should remain the accounting anchor for posted financial amounts. After every controlled refresh or reporting cycle, compare the consolidated transaction population and key financial totals to the corresponding ledger balances. Reconcile by period, entity or office, account, currency where required, and other material reporting dimensions. Differences should be classified: timing differences, valid accounting adjustments, mapping issues, missing transactions, duplicates, or transformation errors. The reconciliation should not simply compare one grand total because two datasets can agree overall while disagreeing materially by program or account. A good model therefore supports both top-level reconciliation to the ledger and detailed drill-back to individual transactions. The process should be documented and approved before reports based on the model are released.

Study instruction:
- "Perform both total-level and dimensional reconciliations so offsetting classification errors cannot remain hidden."
Key point:
- The general ledger remains a critical accounting reference point.
- Consolidated reporting should reconcile at both aggregate and detailed levels.
Official links:
- Power Query Get Data overview — Microsoft Learn
- Excel VBA reference — Microsoft Learn
25. Q: How should budget-versus-actual reporting work when programs and Finance use the same model?
A: Show answer
Budget-versus-actual reporting should be calculated from the common project, donor, account, and period dimensions rather than from separately maintained program and Finance summaries. The model should show approved budget, actual expenditure, variance amount, variance percentage where useful, commitments, forecast, and remaining available budget. Managers should also see the operational explanation for material variances, not only a percentage. For example, underspending may result from delayed procurement, recruitment delays, or activities moving to a later period. Overspending may relate to timing, exchange rates, price changes, coding errors, or genuine resource pressure. Because the same transaction population feeds both Finance and program reports, the discussion can focus on the cause and action instead of arguing over which spreadsheet contains the correct amount.

Study instruction:
- "Link every management variance to an agreed financial figure and an operational explanation."
Key point:
- A shared model turns budget-versus-actual analysis into a common management discussion.
- Variance explanations should connect financial results to program activity and action.

- Power Query documentation — Microsoft Learn
- Overview of Excel tables — Microsoft Support
26. Q: How would the single source of truth improve forecasting?
A: Show answer
Forecasting becomes more useful when the model contains actual expenditure, commitments, remaining activities, planned procurement, known staffing changes, and approved budget revisions. Program managers can then update operational assumptions while Finance validates the financial calculations. The forecast should be separated from historical actuals so users can distinguish what has already happened from what is expected. A controlled workflow can compare the current forecast with the prior forecast, identify material changes, and ask the responsible manager to explain the movement. VBA can automate the preparation of forecast review packs, while Power Query can refresh actuals and approved data before the forecast discussion. The objective is not to make the workbook predict the future automatically; it is to make assumptions visible, consistent, and comparable over time.

Study instruction:
- "Keep actuals, commitments, assumptions, and forecast values as separate measures within one common reporting model."
Key point:
- Forecasting is stronger when financial and operational information are connected.
- A single model supports comparison of forecast versions and explanations of change.

- What Is Power Query? — Microsoft Learn
- Excel VBA reference — Microsoft Learn
27. Q: How should program managers use the consolidated financial information without becoming accounting specialists?
A: Show answer
The model should present management information at the level of decisions that program managers actually control. Instead of exposing every technical accounting field, provide clear measures such as approved budget, actual spend, commitments, forecast, available balance, material variances, and unresolved exceptions. Each measure should have an explanation of what it means and what action is expected from the manager. Finance remains responsible for technical accounting decisions, reconciliations, tax treatment, and financial reporting integrity, while program managers are accountable for operational spending, procurement planning, activity timing, and explanations of program-related variances. Shared definitions allow both groups to discuss the same numbers without requiring program staff to learn every accounting rule. This separation of responsibilities strengthens accountability rather than weakening it.

Study instruction:
- "Design reports around decisions and responsibilities, not around the full complexity of the accounting system."
Key point:
- Program managers need decision-useful financial information, not necessarily full accounting detail.
- Finance should retain responsibility for technical accounting controls and reporting integrity.

28. Q: What should happen when a program disputes the Finance number in the consolidated model?
A: Show answer
The first step should be to avoid changing the reported number simply to resolve the disagreement. Both teams should identify the transaction population, reporting period, coding, and underlying source records used by the calculation. Then determine whether the disagreement concerns an accounting treatment, a missing program record, timing, allocation, an incorrect master-data mapping, or a difference in definition. The model should preserve the evidence so the issue can be resolved systematically. If Finance confirms the accounting figure but the program has a legitimate operational explanation, that explanation should be added as a management commentary rather than changing the accounting number. If Finance identifies an error, the source or transformation rule should be corrected and the resulting impact documented. This keeps the workflow evidence-based rather than driven by informal negotiation over spreadsheets.

Study instruction:
- "Use source data, definitions, and reconciliation evidence to resolve disputes instead of editing reported numbers manually."
Key point:
- Disagreements should be resolved through traceability and documented definitions.
- The report should not become the place where competing interpretations are silently merged.

- What Is Power Query? — Microsoft Learn
- Excel object model overview — Microsoft Learn
29. Q: How would you build an audit trail into a consolidated financial workflow?
A: Show answer
The workflow should record source refresh dates, source file names or system extracts, transformation version where practical, control checks performed, exceptions identified, report generation date, approver, and final output reference. When VBA is used, it can write important workflow events into an audit-log sheet, such as the user, time, batch or period, output created, and result of key validations. The audit trail should explain the process rather than simply record that a macro ran. Source data should also remain identifiable so reviewers can trace a reported total back to the originating records. Approved outputs should be archived separately from working files. This makes later review possible even when the current model has already been refreshed for a new period.

Study instruction:
- "Define the minimum audit evidence needed to reproduce how an approved report was created."
Key point:
- Audit trails should connect source data, transformations, controls, approvals, and final outputs.
- Logging only technical events is not enough without financial traceability.
Official links:
- Excel VBA reference — Microsoft Learn
- Excel object model overview — Microsoft Learn
30. Q: Why should source files and outputs be stored separately?
A: Show answer
Mixing source inputs, working files, and approved outputs creates a high risk of using the wrong version during refresh. A controlled workflow should have separate folders or locations for approved source extracts, working or processing files, exception records, and final reports. The source location should contain only files that are intended for the current process, or the query should contain explicit rules for identifying the approved files. The final output location should preserve dated or versioned copies after approval. Power Query can read from predictable source locations, and VBA can export controlled outputs using standardized naming rules. This structure reduces accidental overwriting and makes the process easier to hand over when another Finance staff member takes responsibility for the reporting cycle.

Study instruction:
- "Review the folder structure and separate approved inputs, processing files, exceptions, and final outputs."
Key point:
- File structure is part of data governance.
- Predictable locations reduce refresh errors and version confusion.

- Power Query documentation — Microsoft Learn
- Excel VBA reference — Microsoft Learn
31. Q: How would you control changes to the financial model itself?
A: Show answer
Changes should be classified into different types because a formatting change is not equivalent to changing an accounting rule. At minimum, distinguish master-data changes, transformation-rule changes, calculation changes, report-layout changes, and VBA-code changes. Material changes should be documented, tested, reviewed, and approved before being used for official reporting. The organization should retain enough version history to determine which logic produced a historical report. VBA can help create controlled release records, but the actual governance process must be independent of the code. Where a change affects a donor report or a key financial measure, test it against a known prior period and document the expected impact. A controlled model is more valuable than an elaborate model that can be changed casually without leaving evidence.

Study instruction:
- "Create a change-control register and classify changes according to financial and operational impact."
Key point:
- Not all workbook changes carry the same risk.
- Changes to accounting logic and transformation rules require stronger review than cosmetic edits.

- Getting started with VBA in Office — Microsoft Learn
- Excel object model overview — Microsoft Learn
32. Q: What testing should be completed before the consolidated workflow goes live?
A: Show answer
Testing should cover both data integrity and management behavior. Use a known period where the organization's totals are already reconciled, then load the same data through the new workflow and compare results. Test missing fields, duplicate transactions, invalid codes, changed file names, additional rows, new programs, new projects, reversals, foreign currency items, interoffice transactions, late invoices, and revised budgets. Verify that exceptions are detected rather than silently accepted. Reconcile the output to the general ledger and key supporting schedules. Also test what happens when a user makes an invalid input or a source file is unavailable. A workflow is not ready merely because the totals look correct under normal conditions; it should also fail safely when the underlying information is incomplete or malformed.

Study instruction:
- "Build a test pack containing normal cases, errors, exceptions, new data, and edge cases before deployment."
Key point:
- Testing should prove both correct output and safe failure.
- Known-period reconciliation is an important baseline for validating the new model.

- Power Query documentation — Microsoft Learn

33. Q: How would you handle a program that submits data in a different format from everyone else?
A: Show answer
Do not redesign the entire reporting model around one unusual input unless there is a sound business reason. First determine whether the program can adopt the common template or data structure. If it cannot, create a controlled transformation layer that maps the exceptional format into the standard model. Document which source fields map to the common fields and which information cannot be supplied. Power Query is useful for this because transformations can convert different source structures into a consistent output without manually rebuilding the dataset each period. The exception should remain visible so the organization knows that the program has a different upstream process. Over time, repeated exceptions can become candidates for process standardization. The aim is to absorb legitimate variation without allowing every program to create its own financial language.

Study instruction:
- "Document all non-standard mappings and review recurring exceptions for process harmonization."
Key point:
- Central reporting can accommodate different source formats through controlled transformations.
- Repeated exceptions should prompt process improvement rather than permanent manual workarounds.
Official links:
- What Is Power Query? — Microsoft Learn
- Power Query Get Data overview — Microsoft Learn
34. Q: How should missing data be treated in a single-source-of-truth workflow?
A: Show answer
Missing data should be classified according to whether it is optional, temporarily unavailable, or essential to the reporting decision. A missing project code on a financial transaction, for example, may prevent reliable donor or program reporting and should normally be an exception rather than being filled with a guessed value. A missing descriptive field may have lower impact. The workflow should therefore include validation rules that identify critical missing fields and stop or flag affected outputs. Power Query can surface nulls and incomplete records during transformation, while VBA can produce a concise exception report for follow-up. Users should never assume that blank means zero. A blank can mean unknown, not applicable, not yet received, or a system failure, and those meanings have very different consequences for management reporting.

Study instruction:
- "Define which fields are mandatory and document the treatment of blank, zero, unknown, and not-applicable values."
Key point:
- Missing data requires interpretation and control.
- Blank, zero, and unknown should never be treated as interchangeable automatically.

- Power Query documentation — Microsoft Learn
- Apply data validation — Microsoft Support
35. Q: How would you ensure that dashboards do not become a second competing source of truth?
A: Show answer
Dashboards should calculate from the controlled reporting model rather than from manually entered headline figures. A dashboard should have clearly defined measures, a visible reporting period, and a link to the underlying transaction or summary tables used for calculation. Users should be prevented from editing calculated outputs unless the design explicitly requires a controlled commentary or forecast input area. When a number looks unusual, the user should be able to trace it to the supporting dataset and source system. Excel tables provide structured references that adjust as data is added or removed, which supports stable formula design. VBA can refresh or prepare the dashboard, but the dashboard itself should remain a presentation layer. This avoids the common situation where the report shown to management is manually adjusted to "look right" even though the underlying accounting data says something different.

Study instruction:
- "Trace every dashboard KPI back to a documented calculation and underlying controlled data source."
Key point:
- Dashboards should display the controlled model, not create a separate version of financial reality.
- Every important KPI needs a clear definition and traceable calculation path.

- Using structured references with Excel tables — Microsoft Support
- Excel VBA reference — Microsoft Learn
36. Q: How would you design the monthly refresh process so that Finance and programs see the same numbers?
A: Show answer
Establish a fixed monthly sequence: close or lock the source period, collect approved source files or system extracts, run validation checks, refresh the consolidated model, reconcile to the general ledger and other control totals, review exceptions, update approved management commentary, and publish the final reporting pack. The reporting period should not continue changing silently while users are reviewing the results. A cutoff timestamp or status field can identify when the dataset became the official reporting version. Programs should receive the same reporting snapshot as Finance rather than generating new numbers from local files after the report has been approved. Power Query supports repeatable refresh of connected sources, while VBA can automate parts of the sequence and produce control check results. The workflow should also define what happens when a late correction is received after the official reporting snapshot.

Study instruction:
- "Document the monthly refresh sequence and identify the exact point when the reporting dataset becomes official."
Key point:
- A common refresh schedule creates a common reporting date and version.
- Late changes require controlled treatment rather than silent updates to published reports.
Official links:
- Power Query Get Data overview — Microsoft Learn
- Excel VBA reference — Microsoft Learn
37. Q: How should the workflow handle late transactions or corrections after the report is published?
A: Show answer
A late transaction should enter through the normal accounting and data process, but the effect on already issued management reports must be documented. The workflow should distinguish between corrections to source accounting records, post-close adjustments, and information that was simply not available at the original reporting cutoff. The reporting model should never overwrite history without an indication that a prior output has changed. Where material, issue a revised report or a clearly identified adjustment. Retain both the original approved output and the revised version, with a reason for the change and an approval record. VBA can help archive the revised output and record the change event. This protects trust in the single source of truth because users can see which version was authoritative at a particular point in time rather than assuming the latest refresh was always the version previously reviewed.

Study instruction:
- "Define a post-close adjustment procedure that preserves both the original and revised reporting states."
Key point:
- A single source of truth still needs historical version control.
- Corrections should be traceable rather than silently rewriting previously approved reports.

38. Q: What common mistakes prevent NGOs from achieving a single source of truth?
A: Show answer
Common failures include allowing different programs to use different codes, manually editing consolidated totals after refresh, maintaining multiple "final" files, importing unapproved source files, mixing budget and actuals in one field, failing to distinguish commitments from expenses, changing formulas without documentation, and allowing dashboards to contain manual numbers that are not linked to the underlying model. Another problem is assuming that automation itself creates control. A fast workflow can simply move bad data faster. The practical response is to establish governance around inputs, definitions, ownership, transformations, exceptions, reconciliations, and report approval. The workbook should make controlled processes easier, not provide a sophisticated way to bypass them. Structured tables, validation rules, Power Query transformations, and documented VBA routines can support the architecture when they are used within that governance framework.

Study instruction:
- "Audit the current workflow for manual overrides, duplicate files, inconsistent codes, undocumented transformations, and unreconciled totals."
Key point:
- Automation does not compensate for weak governance.
- Most single-source-of-truth failures are process and data-management failures rather than formula failures.

- Power Query documentation — Microsoft Learn
- Apply data validation — Microsoft Support
39. Q: How would you measure whether the new financial workflow is actually working?
A: Show answer
Measure operational and control outcomes, not simply whether the workbook runs. Useful indicators include reconciliation differences, percentage of transactions passing validation, number of unresolved exceptions, time spent consolidating data, number of manual adjustments to reports, frequency of duplicate records, number of late source submissions, time required to produce monthly reports, and the number of reports that require post-approval correction. Also monitor whether program managers and Finance are using the same figures during management meetings. A strong workflow should reduce repeated reconciliation work, improve traceability, and make material financial issues visible earlier. User feedback is also important because a technically sound model that staff cannot understand may lead them back to independent spreadsheets. The success measure is therefore whether the organization has one reliable reporting process with clear ownership, not whether it has the most complicated technology.

Study instruction:
- "Create a small set of control and efficiency indicators and compare them before and after implementation."
Key point:
- Measure data quality, control strength, reporting speed, and adoption.
- A successful workflow reduces disagreement and manual repair without reducing review quality.

- Getting started with VBA in Office — Microsoft Learn
40. Q: How would you handle access rights when programs and Finance use the same reporting model?
A: Show answer
Shared reporting should not automatically mean unrestricted access to every underlying field. The workflow should separate information that is appropriate for all program managers from information that is confidential or financially sensitive. For example, a program manager may need project-level expenditure, commitments, budget, and forecast but not individual payroll details or information belonging to another restricted activity. Access should therefore be designed around role and business need. The reporting model should also distinguish between read-only management information and controlled input areas. Where Excel and VBA are used, protect formula and control areas, distribute the workbook through approved storage, and avoid treating workbook protection as a substitute for broader information-security controls. A single source of truth is useful only when users trust both its accuracy and its handling of sensitive information.

Study instruction:
- "Map each report field to the user roles that genuinely need access and separate sensitive information from routine management data."
Key point:
- Common reporting does not require identical access for every user.
- Role-based access should be considered during design, not added after deployment.

41. Q: How would you document the workflow so it survives staff turnover?
A: Show answer
Documentation should explain the purpose of each source, the ownership of master data, the transformation sequence, key calculations, refresh procedure, exception handling, reconciliation steps, approval points, and archive process. A new Finance staff member should be able to determine where source files come from, how to confirm they are approved, what to refresh, what checks to review, and what output is considered final. Keep technical notes separate from user instructions where necessary, but make both accessible from the controlled process. VBA can provide help sheets or controlled prompts, while Power Query query names and transformation steps should use understandable labels instead of cryptic internal terminology. Documentation should also identify known limitations, such as fields that require manual review or sources that cannot be refreshed automatically.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare a handover guide that explains the data flow, control checks, responsibilities, and recovery steps."
Key point:
- A single source of truth is not sustainable if only one person understands how it works.
- Documentation should describe both the business logic and the technical workflow.

42. Q: How would you train program teams to use the consolidated financial workflow correctly?
A: Show answer
Training should focus on the data and decisions that each team controls. Program users should understand the common codes, required fields, budget ownership, commitments, financial reporting dates, exception procedures, and how to interpret the management report. They should also know which information they are expected to correct at source and which issues must be sent to Finance. Training should use real examples from the program environment, such as a procurement transaction with the wrong project code, a delayed activity causing underspending, or a commitment that has not yet appeared as actual expenditure. Finance staff require deeper training on reconciliation, query logic, master data, and exception handling. Repeated user questions should be treated as evidence about weaknesses in the process or documentation, not simply as individual training failures.

Study instruction:
- "Create role-specific training rather than teaching every user the full technical architecture."
Key point:
- Training should reflect each user's responsibilities in the financial workflow.
- Recurring user errors can reveal process-design problems that training alone will not solve.

43. Q: What would you do if programs insist on keeping their own financial trackers?
A: Show answer
First distinguish legitimate operational tracking from duplicate financial reporting. Programs may reasonably need local schedules for activity planning, beneficiary information, procurement follow-up, or field operations. The problem begins when those trackers become competing versions of actual expenditure, budget balances, or donor reporting figures. Agree which measures must come from the controlled financial model and allow local tools to focus on information that the program genuinely owns. Where local trackers contain valuable operational data, integrate those fields into the central model through a defined interface rather than demanding that the program abandon every spreadsheet. The goal is not to eliminate local tools; it is to prevent multiple sources from claiming authority for the same financial measure. This creates a practical balance between operational flexibility and financial control.

Study instruction:
- "Classify each program spreadsheet as an operational tool, source record, or competing financial report and address the risk accordingly."
Key point:
- Local tools can coexist with a central model when their purpose and authority are clear.
- The same financial metric should not have multiple uncontrolled owners.

44. Q: How would you use a consolidated financial workflow during management meetings?
A: Show answer
The management meeting should use the same reporting snapshot that Finance has reconciled and approved. Begin with the overall budget position, then move to material program variances, forecast changes, commitments, cash implications, grant restrictions, and unresolved exceptions. Each significant issue should connect the financial figure to an operational cause and a management action. The report should avoid overwhelming managers with raw transactions unless a specific investigation requires them. Because the model provides a common set of numbers, time can be spent discussing decisions rather than reconciling spreadsheets live during the meeting. A useful workflow can also record agreed actions, responsible owners, and due dates so that financial issues identified during the meeting return into the next reporting cycle as tracked management actions.

Study instruction:
- "Use the consolidated report as the common meeting baseline and record financial actions as part of the management workflow."
Key point:
- The value of a single source of truth is realized when it supports consistent decisions.
- Financial discussions should connect numbers to operational actions and accountability.

45. Q: How would you investigate a situation where the program report and Finance report have the same total but different project balances?
A: Show answer
Equal grand totals do not prove that the underlying classifications agree. I would compare the datasets by project, donor, account, period, transaction type, and other important dimensions rather than stopping at the total. Look for offsetting errors, such as an amount charged to Project A in one report and Project B in another. Then trace differences to source transactions and mapping tables. Check whether allocations, reclassifications, interoffice entries, accruals, or eliminations are treated consistently. The final action should correct the underlying source or mapping where possible rather than changing the project report manually. This type of investigation is an important reason to build a detailed common model: it exposes differences beneath apparently reconciled totals and allows Finance to distinguish data errors from legitimate presentation differences.

Study instruction:
- "When totals agree, test the major reporting dimensions before concluding that the datasets are reconciled."
Key point:
- Aggregate agreement can hide material classification differences.
- Reconciliation should test the dimensions that management relies upon, not only the grand total.
Official links:
- What Is Power Query? — Microsoft Learn
- Using structured references with Excel tables — Microsoft Support
46. Q: How should a single source of truth support donor reporting and internal management reporting at the same time?
A: Show answer
Use one controlled transaction and master-data model but create different reporting views for different purposes. Donor reports may require award-specific classifications, eligible-cost analysis, narrative explanations, and donor-defined periods. Internal management may need organizational totals, forecasts, commitments, unrestricted liquidity, operational variances, and cross-program comparisons. The underlying transactions should remain common, while the reporting rules for each audience should be documented separately. This prevents Finance from maintaining completely independent donor and management datasets that gradually diverge. The same model can also preserve donor-specific mappings where the donor's budget categories do not exactly match the general ledger. That mapping should be controlled and reconciled so that donor reporting does not become a parallel accounting system.

Study instruction:
- "Identify which reporting rules are common and which are audience-specific, then keep both views connected to the same underlying transactions."
Key point:
- Different reports can serve different purposes without creating different transaction populations.
- Donor-specific mappings should remain traceable to the organization's accounting records.

47. Q: How would you design the workflow to scale as the NGO adds more programs and offices?
A: Show answer
Design the model around dimensions and master data rather than separate copies of the workbook for each office. Adding a program should normally mean adding a new approved code and associated reference records, not creating a new reporting architecture. Power Query can combine recurring source files when they follow a known structure, while Excel tables can expand as new rows are added. The reporting calculations should refer to table structures and standard dimensions rather than fixed cell ranges. VBA procedures should work with table objects and named structures where practical so that adding records does not require rewriting the code. As scale increases, review whether Excel remains an appropriate central platform based on transaction volume, concurrent users, security needs, performance, and integration requirements. Scaling should be planned before the workbook becomes a bottleneck.

Study instruction:
- "Test the workflow by adding a hypothetical new program without changing the core reporting logic."
Key point:
- Scalable design adds data and reference values instead of duplicating the whole solution.
- Technology should be reassessed when the organization's scale exceeds the practical limits of the chosen platform.
Official links:
- Using structured references with Excel tables — Microsoft Support
- Power Query documentation — Microsoft Learn
48. Q: What would you do if the central model produces a number that management knows is wrong?
A: Show answer
Do not correct the dashboard by manually typing a replacement figure. Freeze the affected reporting output if necessary, identify the metric definition, trace the number through the calculation, and compare the underlying source population with the authoritative system. Check whether the problem comes from missing source data, incorrect transformation, master-data mapping, duplicate transactions, incorrect formulas, period cutoff, or an accounting adjustment that has not flowed through the model. Then correct the underlying cause, rerun the relevant controls, and document the impact. If the problem affects an already issued report, use the documented revision process rather than silently replacing the file. This approach may require more work in the moment, but it preserves the integrity of the single-source principle because future reports receive the same correction through the controlled workflow.

Study instruction:
- "Trace incorrect outputs back to their root source or transformation instead of editing the final report directly."
Key point:
- The dashboard should never be the primary place where financial errors are repaired.
- Root-cause correction protects future reporting cycles as well as the current report.
Official links:
- What Is Power Query? — Microsoft Learn
- Excel object model overview — Microsoft Learn
49. Q: How would you structure the final financial workflow from transaction capture to management report?
A: Show answer
A practical sequence is: source transaction or operational event; approval and supporting evidence; authoritative system recording; source extraction; data-quality validation; Power Query transformation and standardization; master-data mapping; duplicate and exception checks; consolidation into the controlled reporting model; reconciliation to the general ledger and other control schedules; budget, commitment, and forecast calculations; management review; approval; and controlled report publication and archive. Each stage should have a named owner and a clear entry and exit condition. Finance owns the integrity of accounting outputs, programs own operational information and explanations, and data or systems owners maintain source structures and access. VBA can coordinate repeatable workflow steps, but human review remains necessary for material exceptions and accounting judgments. This sequence creates a chain of evidence from the original activity to the final decision-support report.

Study instruction:
- "Draw the full workflow as a process map and assign an owner and control to every stage."
Key point:
- A single source of truth is the result of a controlled end-to-end process.
- Every stage needs ownership, validation, and traceability.
Official links:
- Power Query Get Data overview — Microsoft Learn
- Excel VBA reference — Microsoft Learn
50. Q: What is the most important design principle when creating a single source of truth across programs and Finance systems?
A: Show answer
The most important principle is to make the organization agree on the meaning, ownership, and lineage of the data before automating the movement of that data. Technology can connect systems, transform files, calculate reports, and produce attractive dashboards, but it cannot decide which program code is correct, which system is authoritative, whether a commitment qualifies under the organization's policy, or who has responsibility for an unexplained transaction. Those decisions need governance. Once the definitions and responsibilities are clear, Power Query can provide repeatable data ingestion and transformation, Excel tables can provide stable structured data areas, validation can improve input quality, and VBA can automate controlled workflow actions and outputs. The resulting single source of truth should allow Finance, programs, management, and donors to see consistent information while still preserving source-system authority, accounting controls, audit evidence, and historical traceability.

Study instruction:
- "Before building automation, document the data definitions, authoritative sources, ownership, control points, reconciliation rules, and reporting outputs."
Key point:
- Governance comes first; automation makes the agreed process repeatable.
- A strong single source of truth combines common definitions, controlled data, reconciliation, traceability, and clear accountability.
Official links:
- What Is Power Query? — Microsoft Learn
- Overview of Excel tables — Microsoft Support
- Excel VBA reference — Microsoft Learn
